--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Mission Analysis:**
- Score decreases by 1 for each item from one half (good half), increases by 1 for each item from the other half (bad half).
- We need robots to collect from the GOOD half and avoid the BAD half.
- The source area has BLACK floor. Nest has WHITE floor. Between is GRAY.
- One wall side is BLUE (good or bad), other is GREEN (good or bad).
- We don't know which color is good yet - the champion uses GoToColor with clr=2 (GREEN walls/objects).

**Champion Analysis:**
- 1 state: GoToColor toward color 2 (GREEN), score -1.71
- This suggests GREEN side is the good (score-decreasing) side.
- The robot keeps going toward GREEN, picks up items there, delivers to nest... but wait, with only 1 state, how does it return to the nest?

Actually, thinking more carefully: the robot moves toward GREEN objects (walls), picks up an item, then... it needs to return to the nest (WHITE floor). With just GoToColor toward GREEN, the robot would stay near the GREEN wall, but the items get delivered when entering the nest. Perhaps the robot oscillates somewhat.

**Strategy:**
- The champion is already decent at -1.71. We need to improve by adding navigation logic.
- Key behaviors needed:
  1. Go to GREEN source (collect good items)
  2. Return to nest (WHITE floor) to deliver
  3. Avoid BLUE source (bad items)

**State designs:**

- 2-state: GoToColor(GREEN) when not on WHITE, GoToColor(RED/nest wall) or GoAwayColor(BLUE) when carrying item
- Better: State0=GoToColor(GREEN), transition on WHITE floor → State1=Exploration/GoToColor(RED)
- The RED wall is on the nest side, so GoToColor(RED) leads to nest.

Let me design multiple FSMs:

1. **Survival**: Copy champion exactly
2. **2-state foraging**: GoToColor(GREEN)[clr=2] → on WHITE floor → GoToColor(RED)[clr=1] → on BLACK floor → back
3. **2-state**: GoToColor(GREEN) → on GRAY/WHITE → GoAwayColor(BLUE)[clr=3]
4. **3-state**: Explore → on BLACK → GoToColor(GREEN) → on WHITE → GoToColor(RED) → on BLACK
5. Various parameter tweaks

Color codes: 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan

FSM designs:

1. Champion copy
2. 2-state: s0=GoToColor(GREEN,clr=2), on WHITE→s1; s1=GoToColor(RED,clr=1), on BLACK→s0
3. 2-state: s0=GoToColor(GREEN,clr=2), on WHITE→s1; s1=GoAwayColor(BLUE,clr=3), on BLACK→s0
4. 2-state: s0=GoToColor(GREEN,clr=2), on WHITE→s1 + prob; s1=GoToColor(RED,clr=1), on GRAY→s0
5. 3-state with exploration
6. 2-state with neighbor conditions
7. GoToColor(RED) only - baseline comparison
8-14. More variations

## Reasoning

**Champion Analysis (score -5.80):**
- State 0: GoToColor(GREEN, clr=2), cyan LED
  - Transition 0: on WHITE floor → State 0 (itself?... wait, D=0 means first OTHER state)
  
Wait, let me re-read. With 3 states, leaving state 0 the others are [state1, state2]: D=0 targets state 1, D=1 targets state 2.

So State 0 (GoToColor GREEN):
- On WHITE floor (p=0.9) → State 1
- Perceives BLUE (clr=3, p=0.85) → State 2

State 1 (GoAwayColor BLUE, clr=3), magenta LED:
- On WHITE floor (p=0.9) → State 0 (D=0 from state1 means first other = state0)
- On BLACK floor (p=0.7) → State 1 (D=1 from state1 means second other = state2)

Wait, from state 1, others are [state0, state2]: D=0→state0, D=1→state2.
- On WHITE (p=0.9) → State 0
- On BLACK (p=0.7) → State 2

State 2 (GoToColor RED, clr=1), off LED:
- On BLACK floor (p=0.9) → State 0 (D=0 from state2 means first other = state0)

**Logic interpretation:**
- State 0: Go toward GREEN source
- When on WHITE (nest): go to State 1 (GoAwayBlue - move away from blue while in nest area)
- When sees BLUE: go to State 2 (GoToColor RED - go toward nest wall)
- State 1: GoAwayBlue (in nest), on WHITE stay/loop back to 0, on BLACK → State 2
- State 2: GoToRed (toward nest), on BLACK → State 0

This makes sense: go to green source, avoid blue, return via red wall.

**Exploitation strategy:**
- Tweak probabilities around the champion
- Try variations: higher/lower p values, different transitions
- Add 4th state for more nuanced behavior
- Try GoAwayColor(BLUE) more aggressively when on BLACK (to avoid picking up bad items)

8-14. Exploration with different structures

Key insight from champion: 
- s0=GoToColor(GREEN) → deliver at nest (WHITE) → s1=GoAwayColor(BLUE) → when on BLACK → s2=GoToColor(RED/nest)
- The flow is: collect green → nest → avoid blue → go to nest wall → collect green again

Variations to try:
- Change p values (0.9→0.95, 0.85→0.9, 0.7→0.8)
- Add transition from s0 to s2 directly on WHITE
- Try 4-state version
- Try with GoToColor(RED) as return behavior instead of GoAwayColor(BLUE)

- On BLACK floor (p=0.7) → State 2 (D=1, second other from s1 = s2... wait, that's s3 with 4 states)

- On WHITE (p=0.9) → State 0

- n2x1: D=2 → s3, c=WHITE (p=0.8)

- On GRAY (p=0.3) → State 0 (D=0, from s3 others are [s0, s1, s2]: D=0→s0)

**Logic:**
- s0: GoToGreen → when at nest (WHITE) go to s1, when sees BLUE go to s2
- s1: GoAwayBlue (in nest area) → when WHITE go back to s0, when BLACK go to s2
- s2: GoToRed (toward nest wall) → when BLACK go to s0, when WHITE go to s3
- s3: Explore → when GRAY go to s0

This is a clever circuit: collect green, return to nest, avoid blue, use red wall to navigate.

**Exploration:** Try different 4-state topologies.

From s2: others = [s0, s1, s3] → D=0→s0, D=1→s1, D=2→s3
From s3: others = [s0, s1, s2] → D=0→s0, D=1→s1, D=2→s2

**State 0:** GoToColor(GREEN=2), cyan LED
- On WHITE (p=0.9) → s1
- Perceives BLUE (l=3, p=0.85) → s2

**State 1:** GoAwayColor(BLUE=3), magenta LED (3 transitions)
- On WHITE (p=0.9) → s0
- On BLACK (p=0.7) → s2
- Perceives BLUE (l=3, p=0.8) → s2 (same destination, reinforcing avoidance)

**State 2:** GoToColor(RED=1), off LED
- On BLACK (p=0.9) → s0
- On WHITE (p=0.8) → s3

**State 3:** Exploration (rwm=10), yellow LED
- On GRAY (p=0.3) → s0

**Logic:** The key innovation in this champion is state 1 has 3 transitions, two of which go to s2 (GoToRed/nest). This creates a strong avoidance of blue + return to nest behavior.

**Key observations for improvement:**
1. The p3x0=0.3 (gray→s0) is quite low - robots spend lots of time exploring
2. s3 could have additional transitions to be more responsive
3. Could try higher p values for s1 transitions
4. The perceive-BLUE → s2 in s1 is good (avoids blue while also going away from it)
5. Could add perceive-GREEN in s3 to go back to collecting

**Exploitation:**
- Try p3x0 = 0.4, 0.5, 0.6
- Add second transition from s3 (e.g., perceive GREEN → s0)
- Tweak p1x1, p1x2 values
- Try p0x0=0.95 (more eager to return on white)
- Try p2x1 = 0.85, 0.9

**Exploration:**
- Try s3 = GoToColor(GREEN) instead of Exploration
- Try 3-state version without s3
- Try different ordering of states

**State 0:** GoToColor(GREEN=2), cyan LED
- On WHITE (p=0.9) → s1
- Perceives BLUE (l=3, p=0.85) → s2

**State 1:** GoAwayColor(BLUE=3), magenta LED (3 transitions)
- On WHITE (p=0.9) → s0
- On BLACK (p=0.7) → s2
- Perceives BLUE (l=3, p=0.8) → s2

**State 2:** GoToColor(RED=1), off LED
- On BLACK (p=0.9) → s0
- On WHITE (p=0.8) → s3

**State 3:** GoToColor(GREEN=2), yellow LED (NEW vs previous champion!)
- On GRAY (p=0.3) → s0
- On BLACK (p=0.5) → s0

So s3 is also GoToColor(GREEN) - a second "go collect" state! When returning from nest (s2 sends to s3 on WHITE), robots go toward green source again. This is essentially a redundant collector state with different exit conditions.

**Key insight:** s3=GoToColor(GREEN) replaces the explorer. This means after delivering at nest, robots immediately go back toward green. The two transitions from s3 (gray→s0, black→s0) both return to s0 which is also GoToColor(GREEN). This seems slightly redundant but might create better timing.

**Exploitation ideas:**
1. Try p3x0=0.4, 0.5 (faster return from s3)
2. Try p3x1=0.6, 0.7 (faster return on black)
3. Add perceive-BLUE transition from s3 → s2 (avoid blue in s3 too)
4. Try p0x0=0.95 (more responsive to white floor)
5. Try p1x1=0.6 or 0.8
6. Add 3rd transition to s2
7. Try s3=GoAwayColor(BLUE) for stronger blue avoidance after delivery

**Exploration ideas:**
- Try s3=GoAwayColor(BLUE) with transition to s0 on gray
- Try different rwm or approach in s3
- Try 3-state version collapsing s0 and s3

## Reasoning

**Final round - must push hard for improvement.**

The champion has held at -10.88 for two rounds. Let me think carefully about what might improve it.

**Champion structure recap:**
- s0: GoToColor(GREEN), exits on WHITE(0.95)→s1 or perceives-BLUE(0.9)→s2
- s1: GoAwayColor(BLUE), exits on WHITE(0.95)→s0, BLACK(0.7)→s2, perceives-BLUE(0.9)→s2
- s2: GoToColor(RED/nest wall), exits on BLACK(0.9)→s0, WHITE(0.8)→s3
- s3: GoToColor(GREEN), exits on GRAY(0.3)→s0, BLACK(0.5)→s0

**Key analysis:** The s3 state is interesting - it's also GoToColor(GREEN) but only exits on GRAY or BLACK floor. This means when the robot is in s3 (which it enters from s2 when on WHITE floor, i.e., at the nest), it goes toward green and transitions back to s0 when on gray (in transit). The p3x0=0.3 and p3x1=0.5 are relatively low, meaning the robot stays in s3 for a while.

**What might help:**
1. Make s3 also exit on WHITE→s1 (so when it reaches nest while in s3, it properly delivers via s1)
2. Try p3x0=0.5, p3x1=0.7 (tested, didn't beat champion)
3. Try making s3 exit perceiving-BLUE→s2 (avoid blue in all collecting states)
4. Try p2x1=0.9 (faster transition to s3 at nest) 
5. Try p1x1=0.8 (more aggressive black-floor escape)
6. Consider: s3 with transition on WHITE→s1 to complete the delivery loop properly
7. Try all p=1.0 equivalent (0.99) for key floor detections

**Unexplored ideas:**
- s3 with WHITE→s1 transition (like s0 has) - creates symmetric behavior
- s2 with perceive-GREEN→s0 transition (go collect when you see green from nest)
- Very high p3x0 (0.7, 0.8) to quickly return to main loop
- Try p0x0=0.99 (almost certain to transition on white)

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

The arena contains a nest area at one end, with a WHITE floor, and a source area at the opposite end, with a BLACK floor. The floor in between is GRAY. The wall on the nest side is RED.

The source area is divided into two halves. The wall blocks behind one half are BLUE and those behind the other half are GREEN.

A robot carries at most one item. Entering a half of the source gives the robot an item of that half's type, replacing any item it was already carrying. Entering the nest with an item delivers it: an item from one half decreases the score by 1, an item from the other half increases it by 1.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
